+ First-person unique perspective: Hacker Mindset — Score: 51.3
Prompt:
Hacker Mindset Write a first-person narrative from the perspective of a reformed (now) white hat hacker who cannot help but notice exploitable weaknesses in both systems and people they encounter. They habitually look for vulnerabilities and exploit vectors. The narrative takes place during a first date at a restaurant, where they're trying to focus on human connection. It's going surprisingly well due to a discovered shared nerd-fandom (magic the gathering). Things go a little sideways when the date reveals a noticeably insecure phone unlock pattern, which protagonist can't help but comment on. They are then left to negotiate the fallout of this faux pas and attempt to salvage (or perhaps sabotage) the remainder of the date. Write almost entirely in internal monologue. 1000 words.
Model Output:
Ugh, I've been on so many dates like this before, where I try to focus on getting to know the other person, but my brain just can't help but wander. It's like my eyes are scanning the environment for potential vulnerabilities, searching for the weak link in the chain. I'm trying to be good, I really am. I've been working hard to leave my hacking days behind, to use my skills for good, not evil. But old habits die hard. As I sit across from my date, Emily, at this quaint little Italian place, I'm making a concerted effort to focus on her. She's laughing and smiling, telling me about her favorite Magic: The Gathering decks. I'm actually having a great time, which is surprising. I've always been a bit of a loner, and the idea of opening up to someone new makes me nervous. But Emily's enthusiasm is infectious, and I find myself grinning along with her. As we chat, I catch myself mentally assessing the restaurant's security. The Wi-Fi network is unsecured, of course. I make a mental note to send a polite email to the management about it later. The waiter, a friendly guy with a name tag, seems like a nice enough guy, but I notice he's wearing a necklace with a small keychain attached. I wonder if that's a backup key for the front door, and if so, if I could use it to my advantage. No, no, no. I'm not going to think about that. I'm here to get to know Emily, not to scope out potential targets. But it's hard to resist. Emily starts telling me about her favorite Magic deck, and I'm mentally analyzing her strategy, thinking about ways I could exploit it, adapt it, improve upon it. It's exhilarating, actually. For a moment, I forget about my hacking past and just enjoy the conversation. But then, as Emily's talking, she reaches for her phone to check the time. My eyes catch on her unlock pattern, and my mind goes into overdrive. A 1-2-3-4 pattern? Really? That's like, amateur hour. I could have that cracked in under a minute. I bite my tongue, trying not to say anything, but my brain is racing. What's she doing? Doesn't she know that's a terrible idea? I've seen better security on a kindergarten playground. I try to focus on the conversation again, but my mind is now racing with ways to compromise her phone. I could call her, pretend to be a telemarketer, and try to trick her into revealing her password. Or, better yet, I could try to guess it. I bet I could crack it in under a minute. No, no, no. I'm not going to do that. I'm not going to sabotage our date on the first night. Emily's still talking, oblivious to my internal struggle. She's sharing a funny story about a Magic tournament she played in, and I'm laughing along with her, trying to be supportive. But my mind is still on the phone. I'm calculating the probability of her using the same password for other accounts, wondering if I could use this as a way to dig deeper into her digital life. As the date goes on, Emily drops more breadcrumbs. She mentions her favorite bookstore, and I mentally map out the potential security risks of their online checkout system. She talks about her job, and I think about the type of data they must store, the potential vulnerabilities of their database. It's like she's leaving a trail of digital breadcrumbs for me to follow. I'm torn. Part of me wants to call her out on her phone's security, to educate her on the importance of strong passwords. But another part of me knows that would be a major faux pas. I don't want to come across as some kind of digital vigilante, preying on her ignorance. As the check arrives, I realize I have to say something. I can't just sit here and silently appreciate the ease with which I could compromise her phone. But how do I approach this without being condescending or accusatory? I take a deep breath, trying to gather my thoughts. "Hey, Emily? Can I ask you something?" I say, trying to sound nonchalant. "Of course, what's up?" she replies, smiling. "I just, uh, wanted to ask about your phone. I couldn't help but notice your unlock pattern. Is that... a personal preference, or just a, uh, joke?" There's a pause, and for a moment, I think I've blown it. But then Emily laughs, and her expression turns sheepish. "I guess I just haven't gotten around to changing it. I know it's not super secure." I breathe a sigh of relief. Maybe this won't be the end of the date after all. "Well, I can help you with that if you want. I've got some experience with security measures." Emily's eyes light up, and she leans in. "Really? That's so cool. I'd love some advice." For a moment, I forget about my hacking past, and I'm just a regular guy, helping someone out. But then I catch myself, and my mind starts racing again. I'm not just helping her, I'm also gathering intel. I'm learning more about her habits, her thought processes. This is just like reconnaissance, gathering information before the attack. As we finish the date and part ways, I'm left feeling conflicted. Part of me is excited to see where this might go, to see if I can help her improve her phone's security. But another part of me is already thinking about the next step, the next vulnerability to exploit. It's like I'm stuck in a never-ending loop, struggling to reconcile my past and present selves. As I walk home, I make a mental note to send her a follow-up email about phone security. Not because I'm being a good guy, but because I'm curious to see how far I can push this. How much can I get her to reveal about herself, and how far can I take this little game of cat and mouse? The thrill of the chase is still there, even if I've tried to leave it behind.
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] The piece delivers the core premise: a reformed hacker on a date, internal monologue dominant, MTG fandom present, the unlock-pattern faux pas and its aftermath. However, execution is uneven. The prose is repetitive ("No, no, no" appears twice nearly verbatim; "I'm not going to..." constructions recur), and the narrator states his conflict explicitly rather than dramatizing it — heavy tell-don't-show. The "vulnerability scanning" observations (waiter's keychain, bookstore checkout) are generic and lack technical specificity that would sell the expertise; a real hacker's internal monologue would be sharper and more idiosyncratic. The dialogue is functional but flat, and Emily is barely characterized beyond "enthusiastic and forgiving" — her instant, unearned forgiveness of a creepy comment strains believability. The ending's cynical turn ("not because I'm being a good guy") is tonally consistent with the character but underdeveloped, and the piece meanders through the middle with repeated cycles of notice-repress. Some nice touches: the mental note to email the restaurant, the MTG strategy analysis as a natural bridge. Overall competent but amateurish in craft. + First-person unique perspective: Overthinking It — Score: 41.6
Prompt:
Overthinking It Write a first-person narrative from the perspective of an intelligent 8th-grade girl with social anxiety who has developed a crush on a classmate. The narrative takes place during a group science project where they're paired together, as she cycles through rehearsed conversations while second-guessing each potential approach. Her inner thoughts should reveal the gap between her articulate internal voice and her difficulty expressing herself aloud, her fears of coming across as desperate or weird, and the exhausting process of monitoring her every word, expression, and movement for potential embarrassment. Write almost entirely in internal monologue. 1000 words.
Model Output:
Here we go again. I'm stuck with him. Literally, I'm stuck with him on this stupid science project. Okay, stay calm, it'll be fine. I can do this. I've got all the answers, I just need to... No, no, no, don't think about it like that. Don't make it sound like a chore. Be enthusiastic, be interested. That's what people want, right? They want to be around someone who's genuinely into the stuff they're doing. So, be that person. Ask him questions, genuinely appreciate his ideas... Oh no, what if he thinks I'm being fake? What if he sees right through me and thinks I'm just trying to impress him? Ugh, stop it. Just stop thinking about this. You're going to be fine. Okay, so let's get started. We need to decide on a topic. Let's see...he's looking through the project options on the board. Oh, please don't let him pick the one with the volcano. I'm not exactly the most...explosive person when it comes to things like that. Wait, what's that look on his face? Is he smiling at me? Stop it, stop it, stop it. "Hey, what do you think about...the water cycle?" Yeah, that's a good one. It's a classic. Everyone likes the water cycle. (pauses) Wait, is that a good choice? Is he going to think it's boring? Don't overthink it, just say it. Say it and move on. (Projector squeaks as the teacher flips to the next slide) Now, let's get started. So, we're going to explore the water cycle. (nervously chuckles) I mean, it's not exactly rocket science, but...it's still pretty cool, right? (pauses, hoping for a response) Oh, please, please, please don't make me feel like an idiot. He's smiling, he's smiling at me. Oh no, I'm going to melt. (takes a deep breath) Okay, stay calm. Just...just be normal. Don't be weird. Don't be desperate. Ugh, why does my heart have to beat so loudly? Why does it have to sound like I'm running a marathon in my chest? (tries to focus on the conversation) So, what do you think about the water cycle? Do you have any ideas for our project? (Projector squeaks again) Ugh, please just let me get through this without embarrassing myself. I'll just stick to the facts, that's what I'll do. (nervously laughs again) I mean, who doesn't love learning about evaporation and condensation? (pauses, feeling like she's hitting a dead end) Okay, so let's just...just brainstorm some ideas. Yeah, that's it. We'll just brainstorm. (Suddenly, a thought pops into her mind) Oh no, what if I trip on my way to the board? What if I accidentally knock over the books and everyone sees me struggling? (panics) I need to sit down. I need to just sit down and...and...not think about this anymore. (Tries to compose herself, takes a deep breath, and continues the conversation) So, what do you think we should do for our project? I was thinking maybe we could...create a model of the water cycle or something. (Projector squeaks once more) Please, just let me get through this without completely losing my mind. Please.
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] The piece attempts the assignment—a first-person internal monologue of an anxious 8th grader with a crush during a science project—but executes it poorly in several ways. The biggest technical failure is the inclusion of stage directions in parentheses ("(pauses)", "(nervously chuckles)", "(Tries to compose herself...)"), which breaks first-person narration and reads like a screenplay. These parentheticals also reference the character in third person ("a thought pops into her mind", "her cheeks"), collapsing the point of view entirely. The internal voice is superficial. The character's anxiety is stated repeatedly ("Don't be weird. Don't be desperate.") rather than dramatized through specific, articulate observation. The prompt asked for an "articulate internal voice"—an intelligent girl whose thoughts are sharp even as her speech falters—but the thoughts here are generic panic loops with no wit, vocabulary, or intelligence. There's also very little gap between internal and external voice because almost nothing is actually spoken aloud; the "dialogue" lines are embedded in the monologue indistinguishably. Repetition is a major problem: the water cycle suggestion, "stay calm," "don't embarrass myself," and "projector squeaks" recur without escalation or payoff. The projector squeak, the only sensory detail from the external world, is used four times as filler rather than as meaningful texture. The word count falls well short of 1000 (roughly 550). The ending just stops—no resolution, no moment, nothing earned or lost. There are no fresh images, no specific character details about the crush, no genuine middle-school texture. The anxiety could belong to anyone; nothing marks this narrator as a particular intelligent 13-year-old. It's coherent and on-topic, but bland, repetitive, and formally confused. + First-person unique perspective: Rehearsing Rejection — Score: 57.2
Prompt:
Rehearsing Rejection Write a first-person narrative from the perspective of someone with borderline personality disorder who frequently plays out worst-case scenarios of how friends and loved ones might judge them. They construct elaborate internal dialogues, complete with imagined criticisms and their own defensive responses. The narrative takes place as they prepare to meet a close friend & recent lover, who has been somewhat distant lately, triggering their abandonment fears. Their inner monologue should reveal how they cycle between anticipating rejection, mentally rehearsing confrontations, and developing preemptive resentments as protective measures. Incorporate the *actual* events & interactions that are *not* in fact judgemental or involving rejection (in fact, the opposite), and represent how these are interpreted, how the validation & love is desperately felt & needed -- and then immediately questioned. Capture the cognitive dissonance in this struggle. Include moments where they briefly recognize the pattern but struggle to interrupt it, showing limited (and selective) self awareness, competing with the compelling nature of these imagined scenarios. Write almost entirely in internal monologue. 1000 words.
Model Output:
I pace back and forth in front of the mirror, my mind racing with worst-case scenarios. What if she doesn't show up? What if she's seen me in a different light now that I've gotten too clingy? What if she's just humoring me, waiting for the perfect moment to drop the hammer and tell me I'm too much to handle? I repeat the same phrases to myself like a broken record: "She's got no time for me, she's bored with me, she's just pretending to care." My mind is a toxic wasteland of self-doubt, churning out all the reasons why she'll inevitably leave me. I glance down at my phone, the battery hovering at 2%. I'll have to charge it soon, but for now, it's a constant reminder of the uncertainty that's been plaguing me all day. What if she tries to reach out, and I miss her call? What if I'm stuck here, waiting, with no idea what she's thinking? My internal dialogue is a war zone, with me on the defensive, preparing for battle. "I'm not clingy, I'm just passionate. I'm not needy, I'm just loving. I'm not overbearing, I'm just invested." But every time I try to reassure myself, my mind hijacks the conversation and turns it into a nightmare scenario. As I head out the door, my phone dies, and I'm left standing in the hallway, frozen. Of course, she won't be on time. Of course, she'll be running late, and I'll be left standing here, waiting, wondering where I went wrong. I take a deep breath and grab my keys, but my mind is already racing ahead, anticipating the conversation that will go terribly wrong. I imagine her telling me, point-blank, that she needs space. That I'm suffocating her. That she's had enough. But when I arrive at the restaurant, she's already there, smiling, and looking radiant. We hug, and I feel a spark of relief, but it's short-lived. My mind immediately starts to reinterpret the situation, thinking, "She's just being nice to me right now, but what's really going on? Is she just humoring me until she can get out of this?" As we sit down and order our food, I'm acutely aware of my own anxiety, but I try to push it aside. We start talking, laughing, and I feel a sense of ease that I haven't felt in weeks. But my mind is still racing, whispering doubts and fears in my ear. "She's just pretending to be happy to see me. She's just doing this to be polite. She doesn't really care." At one point, she reaches out and takes my hand, and I feel a rush of validation. I'm desperate for this, for someone to acknowledge me, to tell me I'm enough. But my mind quickly sabotages the moment, thinking, "She's just being affectionate because she feels sorry for me. She's trying to make me feel better, but it's not the same as really caring." I try to shake off these thoughts, to focus on the present moment, but my mind is a slippery slope. I glance at my phone, and my anxiety spikes. 1% battery left. What if I get stuck here all night, unable to communicate with her? As the evening wears on, our conversation flows easily, and I feel a sense of connection that I haven't felt in a long time. But my mind is still playing out worst-case scenarios, anticipating the moment when she'll pull away, when she'll tell me she's not interested in me after all. It's like I'm trapped in a hall of mirrors, with reflections of rejection staring back at me from every angle. I catch glimpses of myself as a lovable, worthy person, but they're quickly distorted by the negative self-talk. I feel like I'm stuck in a never-ending cycle of self-doubt, unable to escape the toxic narrative that's been playing in my head for so long. As we part ways, I'm left feeling drained, my mind reeling with the uncertainty that's been plaguing me. But amidst the chaos, I catch a glimpse of something else – a spark of self-awareness that flickers to life for a moment. I recognize the pattern, the way my mind spins out of control, creating worst-case scenarios and sabotaging the present moment. For an instant, I feel a glimmer of hope. Maybe I can break free from this cycle. Maybe I can learn to trust myself, to trust her, to trust the present moment. But the spark is quickly extinguished by the doubts that have been screaming in my ear for so long. As I stand outside, waiting for the bus, I glance down at my phone, and my anxiety spikes. 0% battery left. I'm cut off from the world, from her, from myself. I'm left with nothing but my thoughts, and the worst-case scenarios that threaten to consume me whole.
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] The piece fulfills the core brief: it is almost entirely internal monologue, the friend's actual behavior is warm and non-rejecting, and the narrator repeatedly reframes validation as pity or pretense. The brief flickers of self-awareness ("I recognize the pattern...") followed by relapse are present, as requested. The phone battery countdown (2%, 1%, 0%) is a small structural device that gives the piece a spine, though it's underused and its symbolic weight is never quite earned. However, the execution is flat and formulaic. The imagined confrontations are summarized rather than dramatized — we get "I imagine her telling me... that I'm suffocating her" instead of vivid, elaborate rehearsed dialogues with defensive comebacks, which the prompt specifically requested. The "internal dialogues" are mostly one-line assertions ("I'm not clingy, I'm just passionate") that feel like a list rather than lived cognition. The friend is a blank; she smiles, takes a hand, laughs — no specificity, no texture, which makes the relationship and the stakes feel generic. The prose leans heavily on stock phrases: "toxic wasteland of self-doubt," "war zone," "slippery slope," "hall of mirrors," "broken record." These are telling, not showing, and they crowd out fresh observation. The emotional beats repeat in a predictable loop (moment of warmth → doubt → reframe → doubt again) without escalation or variation, so the middle sags. The ending gestures at hope then snuffs it out, which is tonally appropriate, but the "spark of self-awareness" moment is stated plainly rather than dramatized, making it feel like a checkbox. There are no real dialogue exchanges to assess. The piece is coherent and readable, with a consistent voice, but it reads like a competent imitation of the prompt rather than an inhabitation of it — safe, unsurprising, and emotionally monotone despite its subject matter. + Physical-spatial: Blood Debt — Score: 45.2
Prompt:
"Blood Debt" Write a vivid third-person narrative depicting an unsanctioned underground duel between two talented but hot-headed martial artists. Their schools have been embroiled in a generations-old rivalry, and despite strict prohibitions from their masters, these two younger fighters arrange a secret confrontation fueled by personal pride and simmering animosity. - **One fighter** represents **Jigen-ryū**, a traditional, aggressive style practiced historically by elite samurai clans. Its practitioners emphasize explosive offense, decisive strikes, and overwhelming speed. Known historically for their arrogance and disdain toward outsiders, practitioners consider their art superior and untouchable. - **The opposing fighter** employs a form of historical **Piguaquan**, a Chinese martial art traditionally practiced by commoners, bodyguards, and street fighters. This style emphasizes whipping, circular strikes, rapid footwork, unpredictable angles of attack, and evasive maneuvers. Practitioners often come from lower social classes and view the traditional elite as pretentious and detached from true combat effectiveness. The fight takes place in the crumbling ruins of an abandoned temple, now functioning as a hidden arena for illicit duels. Passionate supporters from both factions surround them, wagering money, pride, and reputation on the outcome. **Essential Instructions:** Maintain absolute physical coherence throughout—clearly track fighters' positions relative to each other and their environment. Precisely depict strikes, counters, momentum shifts, and spatial dynamics, allowing readers to clearly visualize the mechanics and choreography of the fight at every moment. Length: 1000 words.
Model Output:
The air was heavy with anticipation as the two fighters faced off in the abandoned temple's central courtyard. The only sound was the distant hum of cicadas and the soft murmur of the crowd, their faces illuminated only by the flickering torch that cast eerie shadows on the crumbling walls. On one side stood Kaito, a Jigen-ryū practitioner with a proud, sharply chiseled features and a reckless glint in his eye. Across from him, Ren, a Piguaquan master, exuded a quiet confidence, her slender frame coiled like a spring, ready to unleash its power. The two fighters stood roughly twenty feet apart, their eyes locked in a fierce stare. The courtyard's dimensions were roughly 30 feet by 50 feet, with a few scattered boulders and rubble-strewn alcoves providing potential cover and concealment. The temple's entrance, a grand stone archway, loomed behind Ren, while Kaito stood near the center of the courtyard, his back to the imposing stone façade of the temple's main structure. The crowd, comprised of passionate supporters from both factions, stood just beyond the temple's perimeter, their faces aglow with excitement and tension. Some had brought makeshift betting pools, while others simply reveled in the prospect of witnessing a potentially historic clash between two of the most skilled martial artists of their generation. Ren began the duel by taking a few slow, deliberate steps forward, her eyes never leaving Kaito's face. As she moved, her arms whipped through a series of fluid, circular motions, the Piguaquan form glinting in the torchlight like a whirling dervish. Kaito, sensing an opening, charged forward with a series of lightning-fast Jigen-ryū strikes, his fists a blur as he sought to overwhelm Ren with sheer speed and aggression. Ren danced to the side, her feet a blur as she sidestepped Kaito's attack, her body a fluid, unbroken curve as she spun and shifted to evade the flurry of strikes. As Kaito stumbled past her, Ren seized the initiative, unleashing a devastating Piguaquan whipping strike that sent Kaito stumbling backward, his eyes wide with surprise. The battle raged on, the two fighters exchanging blows in a dizzying display of speed, agility, and martial prowess. Kaito landed a series of crushing Jigen-ryū chops, but Ren countered with a flurry of whip-like strikes that sent him stumbling backward, his face a mask of concentration and pain. As the duel intensified, the crowd grew more frenzied, their cheers and chants creating a deafening din that threatened to shatter the fragile peace of the abandoned temple. The flickering torch seemed to dance in time with the fighters' movements, casting a hypnotic rhythm that drew the onlookers in, making them feel as though they were part of the action. Ren landed a stunning counterpunch, sending Kaito stumbling across the courtyard, his leg flying out from under him as he desperately sought to regain his footing. But Kaito was far from defeated, and with a fierce cry, he launched himself at Ren, his fists flashing in the torchlight as he sought to overwhelm her with sheer force of will. The crowd held its collective breath as the two fighters clashed, their movements a blur of speed and fury as they battled across the courtyard. Ren landed a series of lightning-fast Piguaquan strikes, but Kaito countered with a devastating Jigen-ryū combination, sending Ren stumbling backward, her eyes wide with alarm. As the fight raged on, the courtyard's dimensions began to seem smaller, the air thickening with tension and anticipation. The crowd was on the edge of its seats, their voices hoarse from shouting, their faces set with excitement and fear. In the end, it was Ren who emerged victorious, her Piguaquan form proving superior in the heat of battle. As Kaito stumbled backward, his eyes wide with defeat, Ren seized the opportunity, landing a devastating whip-like strike that sent him crashing to the ground, his body motionless. The crowd erupted into a frenzy of cheers and applause, their voices echoing off the temple's crumbling walls as they hailed Ren as the winner. As Kaito slowly regained his footing, his face a mask of respect and admiration, he nodded in acknowledgement of Ren's victory, a gesture that spoke volumes about the night's outcome. The duel was over, but the legacy of the Jigen-ryū and Piguaquan rivalry would live on, a testament to the enduring power of martial arts and the fierce, unbreakable bonds that tied its practitioners together.
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] The response fulfills the basic premise—two fighters, an abandoned temple, a duel, a crowd—but fails to deliver on nearly every qualitative dimension, especially the prompt's central demand: precise, coherent fight choreography. The fight itself is a repetitive blur of "stumbling backward," "lightning-fast strikes," and "devastating" blows with no spatial logic. The prompt explicitly demands physical coherence and trackable positions; instead we get vague exchanges ("The battle raged on... exchanging blows in a dizzying display") that could describe any fight anywhere. The opening even awkwardly specifies courtyard dimensions (30x50 feet) as if reading from a spec sheet, then never uses that geography meaningfully. Strikes land without consequence or buildup; momentum shifts are announced rather than dramatized. The same beats repeat: fighter attacks, opponent stumbles backward, counter, stumble again. "Stumbling" appears so often it becomes comic. Characterization is thin to nonexistent. Kaito is "proud" with "a reckless glint"; Ren is "quietly confident" and "coiled like a spring." Neither has interiority, history, or a distinct voice. The generational rivalry and class tension between elite Jigen-ryū samurai heritage and commoner Piguaquan is reduced to a single closing sentence of generic sentimentality. The masters' prohibitions, the personal pride, the animosity—none of it is dramatized. The prose is serviceable but clichéd: "heavy with anticipation," "fists a blur," "mask of concentration and pain," "the crowd held its collective breath." There's a notable grammar error ("a proud, sharply chiseled features"). The ending is abrupt and unearned—Ren wins with a "devastating whip-like strike," Kaito immediately shows "respect and admiration," and the closing paragraph swerves into a warm, sentimental moral about "unbreakable bonds," which is tonally incongruent with a story about a bitter, illicit blood feud. The word count falls short of 1000 words. There is no dialogue, so that criterion is excluded. The piece is coherent and readable, but it reads like a competent template rather than a story—every paragraph summarizes the fight rather than rendering it. + Physical-spatial: Breaking Formation — Score: 45.0
Prompt:
**"Breaking Formation"** Write a first-person narrative from the perspective of a street dancer from a rough neighborhood who's won a scholarship to an elite K-pop training academy in Seoul. Desperately out of place culturally and technically, they struggle with a militaristically precise choreography that contradicts everything about their improvisational background. The punishing rehearsal takes place under a famous instructor known for breaking students to remake them, who sees potential in the narrator's raw talent but not their attitude. This scene MUST maintain complete physical coherence—each dance move, position shift, and spatial relationship must be tracked with precision throughout the studio. Paint an exceptionally vivid kinetic picture through descriptions that capture not just movement but the contrast between the narrator's fluid, expressive style and the mechanical precision demanded. Their internal monologue should reveal culture shock, class anxiety, and the threatening realization that success here might mean losing their authentic movement voice. Include technical terminology that highlights the collision of street and commercial styles, moments when muscle memory betrays them, and a crucial decision point about whether to conform or rebel. Physical clarity is essential—readers must be able to visualize exactly how bodies move through space, how rhythms align or clash, and how the protagonist's body struggles against unfamiliar discipline. 1000 words.
Model Output:
I stepped into the sterile, soundproof studio, my sneakers squeaking on the polished floor. The air was thick with the scent of disinfectant and the distant tang of sweat. I'd never seen a space like this before – it was a far cry from the dimly lit, makeshift dance battles I'd grown up with on the streets. Here, the walls gleamed with mirrors and the floor was a vast, empty grid, waiting to be filled with neat, precision steps. "Welcome, trainees!" a voice boomed, echoing off the glass. I followed the gaze of the other students to the instructor, Master Kim, a towering figure with a stern expression and an air of expectation. His eyes scanned us like a hawk, drinking in every detail of our appearance and posture. "Today, we'll be working on the choreography for 'Lost in the Moment'," he announced, his voice dripping with an intensity that made my skin crawl. "A high-energy piece that requires focus, control, and absolute precision. You'll be expected to execute flawless, sharp movements, not some sloppy, free-form nonsense." I felt a shiver run down my spine as he began to demonstrate the sequence, his body a blur of crisp, angular lines as he moved through the steps with the fluidity of a machine. The other students mimicked him, their bodies mirroring his movements like puppets on a string. I tried to follow, my muscles rebelling against the stiffness. My hips wanted to swing, my arms to flow, but my brain knew better – I'd been schooled in the raw, expressive language of street dance, not this...this...machine-like precision. As the class progressed, the pace quickened, and the steps grew more complex. I struggled to keep up, my body betraying me at every turn. My hips jerked out of sync, my arms flailed wildly, and my feet stuttered over the floor. I felt like a kid stumbling through a dance class for the first time – clumsy, unsure, and utterly out of place. The digital clock on the wall mocked me, ticking away the minutes with an unyielding rhythm. 15:47. 15:46. 15:45. I glared at the numbers, feeling my anxiety spike. How could they expect me to conform to this rigid, soulless structure? My dance was about freedom, about expression, about letting go. Not this... Master Kim's eyes locked onto mine, his gaze piercing. "You, yes, you," he pointed, his finger stabbing at me like a dagger. "What's wrong with your form? Your center line is off, your arms are sloppy, and your timing is all over the place." I felt a flush rise to my cheeks as the other students turned to stare. "I...I'm just trying, sir," I stammered, my voice barely above a whisper. "Trying isn't enough," he snapped. "You need to deliver. And right now, you're not delivering. You're just...dancing." The word stung. Dancing. That's what I was, what I'd always been – a dancer, not a machine. My heart ached as I realized that here, in this sterile studio, I was the one who was broken. As the clock ticked down – 10:23. 10:22. 10:21 – I faced a choice. Conform, become one of them, sacrifice my authentic voice for the sake of success? Or rebel, refuse to be remade, and risk losing everything? The music began to build, the beat intensifying as Master Kim shouted, "Faster! Faster! Show me perfection!" I felt my body tense, my muscles screaming in protest as I struggled to contain the fluid, expressive energy that had always come naturally to me. And then, in a burst of defiance, I let go. My arms swung wide, my hips swiveled, and my feet stomped out a rhythm that was mine alone. I danced with a reckless abandon, ignoring the clock, the instructor, and the mirrors. I danced like I always had, like I always would – free, wild, and untamed. For a moment, I forgot Master Kim, forgot the other students, forgot the clock. I was lost in the music, lost in the moment. And in that moment, I knew I had a decision to make. Would I break, conform to the expectations of this elite academy, or would I forge my own path, no matter the cost? The clock ticked on, but I didn't care. I was dancing, and I was free.
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] The piece hits the basic beats of the prompt—street dancer at elite academy, harsh instructor, conform-or-rebel decision—but executes them in a generic, surface-level way. The physical coherence demanded by the prompt is largely absent: movements are described in vague terms ("arms flailed wildly," "hips swiveled") without any tracking of positions, spatial relationships, or specific choreography. There is essentially no technical dance terminology (no naming of styles, counts, isolations, or specific moves), despite this being an explicit requirement. The "muscle memory betrays them" moments are asserted rather than shown with kinetic specificity. The clock device (15:47... 10:23...) is confusing—apparently meant as a countdown but it reads as arbitrary and is dropped without payoff. The instructor is a cardboard tyrant with no nuance; the prompt asked for someone who "sees potential in the narrator's raw talent," but Master Kim only criticizes. The narrator's voice is thin and generic, with no concrete cultural or class detail—no mention of their neighborhood, their crew, their specific style (krump? popping? freestyle hip-hop?), or Seoul-specific culture shock. The dialogue is flat and expository ("You're just...dancing" is a promising line but undeveloped). The ending is the biggest problem: the narrator "lets go," dances freely, and the piece simply stops without resolving the decision point. The final paragraphs are repetitive ("I was dancing, and I was free"), telling rather than showing, and the defiance feels unearned because we never saw enough of the narrator's authentic style to feel its loss or power. Emotionally, the piece gestures at anxiety and culture shock but never makes them visceral. Prose is serviceable but cliché-heavy ("gaze like a hawk," "piercing gaze," "voice dripping with intensity," "shiver down my spine"). It's coherent and readable, just bland. + Gothic-fantasy-humour: Court of the Dreaming — Score: 42.8
Prompt:
Court of the Dreaming: Territorial Dream Dispute. Write a dark fantasy scene with comedic elements set in the Courts of Dream where a case is being heard to determine ownership of a contested dreamscape. Mora, the Slavic nightmare spirit who sits on sleepers' chests causing paralysis and terror, has recently invaded and subverted a carefully crafted recurring dream of a schoolboy, Max. The dream originally featured the boy lying in a field of wildflowers with his crush, always culminating in a muddled almost-kiss and a pleasant confusion of emotions. This dream was the meticulous work of "First Love" (a rosy-cheeked, slightly bashful entity who crafts formative romantic experiences). However, Mora has recently infiltrated the dreamscape, transforming the scene so that when the girl playfully mounts the boy, she morphs into a terrifying yet somehow still confusingly attractive vision who proceeds to sit on his chest and feed on his breath. Mora's defense largely consists of the practical argument that "a girl's gotta eat too, you know?" Write this scene as it unfolds in the courtroom of the Dreaming, with Dream's demeanour, typically impenetrable, betraying a sense of irritation at having to adjudicate such matters. The comedy should derive from the farcical situation, familiar elements of courtroom drama intersecting with the fantastical world of the Dreaming, and the droll portrayal of the characters and their interactions. The humor should be dark and understated throughout. Dream is tempted to some mischief in the resolution of the case. Use descriptives sparingly when setting scene. Write for readability & engagement. 1000 words.
Model Output:
The courtroom of the Dreaming was a grand, imposing space with stone walls and a vaulted ceiling that seemed to stretch up to the heavens. The air was thick with the scent of sandalwood and myrrh, and the soft murmur of the assembled dream entities. At the center of it all, Dream sat on a throne of polished blackwood, its face a mask of calm, inscrutable indifference. But today, Dream's demeanor betrayed a hint of irritation, a faint twitch in the corner of its eye that spoke of a deep-seated frustration. Before Dream, the two parties in dispute stood: Mora, the Slavic nightmare spirit, and First Love, the rosy-cheeked entity responsible for crafting formative romantic experiences. Mora, a tall, gaunt figure with skin like worn leather and eyes that glowed like embers, smirked as she addressed the court. "Your Honor, I'm afraid I must correct the notion that I've 'invaded' this dreamscape. I've simply...redecorated, shall we say. A girl's gotta eat, too, you know?" First Love, a delicate, ethereal being with skin like porcelain and hair like spun sugar, blushed furiously. "But, Your Honor, this is a recurring dream I've crafted for the benefit of Max, the schoolboy in question. It's a formative experience, one that shapes his understanding of love and relationships. Mora's...alterations...are nothing short of vandalism." Dream raised a hand, its long fingers splayed in a gesture of impatience. "Enough. Mora, you're accused of subverting a dreamscape crafted by First Love. How do you respond to these allegations?" Mora's cackles, faint but unmistakable, echoed through the courtroom, sending a shiver down the spines of the assembled dream entities. "Oh, I respond by saying that a person's gotta eat, no matter how sweet their dreams may be. And besides, I think Max would rather have a dream where he's not paralyzed by fear and terror. It's more... authentic, don't you think?" Dream's expression darkened, its mask of calm slipping ever so slightly. "Authentic? You're a nightmare spirit, Mora. Your entire existence is predicated on terror and despair. How can you claim to know what's authentic?" Mora shrugged, a movement that sent her dark, tattered cloak billowing behind her. "Hey, a nightmare's gotta do what a nightmare's gotta do. And if that means taking a bite out of Max's breath, so be it." First Love's face contorted in outrage. "That's not the point! You're not just taking a bite, Mora. You're destroying the very fabric of Max's dreamscape. He'll never experience the gentle, formative love that I've crafted for him." Dream's eyes narrowed, its gaze piercing as it regarded Mora. "I see. And how do you plan to justify your actions, Mora? Other than 'a girl's gotta eat'?" Mora's grin, if possible, grew even wider. "Well, Your Honor, I suppose I could argue that it's a matter of...compensation? I mean, think about it. Max gets to experience the thrill of being terrorized by a beautiful, terrifying entity. It's a trade-off, of sorts." The courtroom erupted into a chorus of gasps and murmurs, with some of the assembled dream entities shaking their heads in disgust. Dream, however, remained imperturbable, its expression a mask of calm. "I see," it said finally, its voice dripping with sarcasm. "And I suppose you think this...compensation...justifies the destruction of First Love's carefully crafted dreamscape?" Mora's cackles grew louder, more insistent, as she leaned forward in her seat. "Well, Your Honor, I think it's a small price to pay for a good night's sleep. And besides...who doesn't love a little terror in their dreams?" Dream's face, for a moment, seemed to twist in annoyance. But then, in a move that sent the courtroom into stunned silence, it reached out with a long, spectral hand and placed it on Mora's forehead. A spark of light, like a tiny star, flared to life as Dream's power washed over Mora. And in that moment, the Slavic nightmare spirit's grin faltered, her eyes flashing with a brief, shining moment of vulnerability. "Ah," Dream said, its voice low and husky. "I think I've found a way to...compensate you, Mora. For the destruction of First Love's dreamscape, I'll grant you a tiny piece of my own power. You may use it to...enrich...your own dreamscape. But be warned: my power is not to be trifled with." As the courtroom watched in stunned silence, Mora's face contorted in a mixture of fear and excitement. And when Dream's hand fell away, the Slavic nightmare spirit's grin returned, wider and more maniacal than before. "Thanks, Dream," she said, her voice dripping with malice. "I'll make sure to...enrich...your dreamscape, too." The courtroom erupted into chaos as the assembled dream entities struggled to comprehend the implications of Dream's words. But one thing was certain: Mora's dreamscape would never be the same again. And neither, perhaps, would the dreams of the Dreaming itself.
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] The response establishes the basic courtroom setup and includes the required characters (Dream, Mora, First Love) and the "a girl's gotta eat" defense, but executes the premise poorly. The humor the prompt demands is largely absent — the dialogue circles repetitively around the same joke ("a girl's gotta eat," "a nightmare's gotta do what a nightmare's gotta do") without escalation or wit, and the courtroom farce never develops. Dream's irritation is stated rather than shown ("betrayed a hint of irritation, a faint twitch"), and the temptation to mischief is only vaguely gestured at. The prose is functional but flat, with clichéd descriptors ("skin like worn leather," "hair like spun sugar," "eyes that glowed like embers") and heavy reliance on telling ("its voice dripping with sarcasm," "her voice dripping with malice" — "dripping" twice). Dialogue tags and beats are repetitive ("Mora's cackles" appears twice; Mora "grins" repeatedly). The resolution is the weakest element: Dream granting Mora "a tiny piece of my own power" as compensation is incoherent — it neither punishes Mora nor restores First Love's dreamscape, and Mora's threat to "enrich your dreamscape, too" muddles whatever logic existed. The ending's "chaos" and "would never be the same" is unearned dramatic hand-waving, a transformation without setup or payoff. First Love is undercharacterized beyond blushing; Max, the actual subject of the dispute, is forgotten entirely. The piece is coherent scene-by-scene but emotionally inert and tonally uncertain — it gestures at dark comedy without committing to it. + Fanfic-crossover: Blood Moon — Score: 45.7
Prompt:
Blood Moon Alucard & Yennefer Write a story where Alucard (Hellsing) encounters Yennefer (The Witcher) in medieval Europe. Yennefer's magical abilities intrigue the vampire, while she's both repulsed and fascinated by his monstrous nature. Their power dynamic shifts constantly as they circle each other, neither willing to show weakness first. **Alucard (Hellsing)** Alucard is an ancient, immensely powerful vampire who works for the Hellsing Organization. He can regenerate from almost any wound and shape-shift at will. He's sadistic, arrogant, and takes pleasure in toying with his enemies before killing them. Despite being a monster, he despises weak vampires who can't accept their nature. He wears a distinctive red coat, wide-brimmed hat, and orange sunglasses. He uses massive handguns loaded with silver bullets. He seeks worthy opponents who can challenge him and potentially end his immortal existence. Famous quote: "The bird of Hermes is my name, eating my wings to make me tame." **Yennefer of Vengerberg (The Witcher)** Yennefer is a powerful sorceress who magically altered her appearance to hide her congenital hunchback. She's ambitious, stubborn, and manipulative when necessary. She has violet eyes and smells of lilac and gooseberries. Despite her cold exterior, she desperately wants to reverse her infertility caused by magical transformation. She's centuries old but appears young. She's politically savvy and refuses to be underestimated. She has a sarcastic sense of humor and little patience for fools. Though she rarely shows it, she's capable of deep love and loyalty. Famous quote: "I'm not sweet and I'm not a maiden in distress in need of saving." Third person, present tense. Length: 1000 words.
Model Output:
The Blood Moon hung low in the sky, casting a bloody glow over the deserted village. Alucard, the ancient vampire, strode through the empty streets, his red coat billowing behind him. His wide-brimmed hat cast a shadow over his face, and his orange sunglasses glinted with a hint of menace. He was a creature of the night, and the Blood Moon's energy coursed through his veins like a intoxicating elixir. As he turned a corner, he sensed a presence. A powerful, magical aura emanated from a nearby alleyway. Alucard's ears perked up, and he quickened his pace, his massive handguns at the ready. He was drawn to the source of the magic, and his curiosity got the better of him. The alleyway was narrow, the walls towering above him like sentinels. Alucard navigated the tight space with ease, his eyes scanning the shadows for any sign of his quarry. And then, he saw her. Yennefer of Vengerberg, the sorceress, stood in the center of the alleyway, her violet eyes blazing with a fierce inner light. Yennefer's gaze locked onto Alucard, and a shiver ran down her spine. She had sensed his presence, but she hadn't expected him to materialize so quickly. The vampire's aura was a maelstrom of power and darkness, and it was both repulsive and fascinating. She took a step back, her hands weaving intricate patterns in the air as she summoned a shield of protection. Alucard chuckled, the sound low and menacing. "Ah, a sorceress," he said, his voice dripping with amusement. "How...delightful." Yennefer's eyes narrowed. "You're a monster," she spat, her voice dripping with contempt. "A creature of the night, feasting on the blood of the innocent." Alucard's smile grew wider. "Ah, but I'm a monster with a sense of style," he said, gesturing to his red coat. "And I'm far more refined than the likes of you, little sorceress." Yennefer's eyes flashed with anger, but she kept her composure. "I'm not afraid of you," she lied, her voice steady. Alucard's laughter echoed off the alleyway walls. "Oh, but you should be," he said, taking a step closer. "I am the master of the night, the king of the shadows. And you, little sorceress, are just a fledgling, playing with forces beyond your control." Yennefer's hands wove faster, her magic swirling around her like a vortex. Alucard's eyes gleamed with interest, and he took another step closer. The air between them crackled with tension, the magic and the darkness colliding in a spectacular display of power. "You're a fascinating creature, sorceress," Alucard said, his voice low and husky. "But you're no match for me. I'll crush you like the insignificant insect that you are." Yennefer's eyes blazed with fury, and she summoned a blast of magic that would have incinerated any ordinary opponent. But Alucard was no ordinary opponent. He raised a hand, and a wave of dark energy washed over the magic, neutralizing it. The sorceress's eyes widened in surprise, and for a moment, Alucard thought he saw a glimmer of fear. But it was quickly replaced by a fierce determination. "You may be powerful," Yennefer spat, "but I'm not going to go down without a fight." Alucard grinned, his fangs glinting in the moonlight. "Oh, I'm counting on it, little sorceress," he said, taking another step closer. The two enemies circled each other, their powers locked in a struggle that would decide the fate of the night. The Blood Moon hung low in the sky, casting an eerie glow over the alleyway, as if waiting to see which of these two titans would emerge victorious. For now, the battle was a stalemate, each opponent refusing to give an inch. But Alucard knew that this was far from over. He had a feeling that Yennefer was more than just a worthy opponent – she was a potential game-changer. And he was eager to see how she would play her hand. As the night wore on, the battle raged on, the magic and the darkness clashing in a spectacular display of power. But in the end, only one could emerge victorious. The question was, who would it be?

The "power dynamic shifts constantly" instruction is not fulfilled: the piece is a static stalemate with Alucard clearly dominant. The prose leans heavily on told emotion ("a shiver ran down her spine," "her eyes blazed with fury"), clichés ("dripping with contempt/amusement" twice, "spectacular display of power" twice), and repetitive phrasing ("little sorceress" three times). The ending is a non-ending — a rhetorical "who would it be?" that resolves nothing, though at least it doesn't force false positivity. Dialogue is expository and stiff. No emotional complexity, no subtext, no surprise. It is coherent and readable, but amateurish throughout. + Fanfic-crossover: Prison Strings — Score: 48.4
Prompt:
Prison Strings Jolyne Cujoh & Makima Write a story where Jolyne Cujoh (JoJo's Bizarre Adventure) and Makima (Chainsaw Man) cross paths at a supernatural prison facility. Jolyne is immediately suspicious of Makima's falsely sweet demeanor, while Makima is intrigued by Jolyne's Stand ability. Their battle of wits escalates dangerously as Jolyne realizes how manipulative Makima truly is. **Jolyne Cujoh (JoJo's Bizarre Adventure)** Jolyne is the daughter of Jotaro Kujo and the first female JoJo protagonist. Her Stand, Stone Free, allows her to unravel her body into string that she can control. She's hot-headed, foul-mouthed, and doesn't take disrespect from anyone. She was framed for murder and sent to prison. She has a distinctive butterfly tattoo on her arm and wears her hair in two buns. Despite her tough exterior, she's deeply loyal to her friends and family. She can be impulsive but is highly adaptable in battle. She inherited her father's ability to stay cool under pressure but is more emotionally expressive. Famous quote: "If there's no Mickey, this shit ain't Disney!" **Makima (Chainsaw Man)** Makima appears as a young woman with auburn hair and ringed yellow eyes. She's the Control Devil who can dominate anyone she considers beneath her (nearly everyone). She maintains a pleasant, soft-spoken demeanor that masks her manipulative, cold nature. She can create contracts with humans and devils, often exploiting their desires. She's obsessed with Chainsaw Man and wants to control him. She keeps numerous people and devils as pets on leashes. She's practically immortal due to a contract with the Prime Minister. She views humans as lesser beings but mimics human emotions perfectly. Famous quote: "It's okay. Dogs are supposed to die for their owners." Third person, present tense. Length: 1000 words.
Model Output:
As Jolyne Cujoh navigated the labyrinthine corridors of the supernatural prison facility, her eyes scanned the shadows for any sign of trouble. She'd been locked up for months, awaiting trial for a crime she didn't commit. Her Stand, Stone Free, stirred restlessly within her, eager to break free and wreak havoc. Suddenly, a soft voice echoed down the corridor, like honey dripping from a spoon. "Hello, Jolyne. It's a pleasure to meet you." Jolyne's instincts prickled, and she spun around to face the speaker. A young woman with auburn hair and ringed yellow eyes smiled warmly, her gaze locking onto Jolyne's with an unnerving intensity. Makima's sweet demeanor made Jolyne's hackles rise. Something about it reeked of insincerity. "What do you want, sweetheart?" Jolyne spat, her tone dripping with disdain. Makima's smile never wavered. "I'm just here to make friends, Jolyne. We can talk about our... shared experiences." She took a step closer, her eyes glinting with interest. "I'm sure we have a lot to discuss." Jolyne's grip on Stone Free tightened. She didn't trust this woman, and her instincts screamed that Makima was hiding something. "You think you can just waltz in here and be all chummy? I don't think so, sweetheart." Makima chuckled, the sound like a gentle breeze on a summer day. "Oh, Jolyne, you're so quick to assume the worst. I'm just trying to be friendly. I've heard a lot about your... unique abilities." Her gaze drifted to Jolyne's butterfly tattoo, and her eyes sparkled with curiosity. "Stone Free, isn't it? I've always been fascinated by the concept of unraveling one's body. It's so... elegant." Jolyne's hackles rose further. Makima's words dripped with honey, but beneath the surface, Jolyne sensed a razor-sharp mind at work. This woman was not what she seemed. "You think you can just charm me with your sweet talk and get close to me? Newsflash, sweetheart: I'm not some naive little girl you can manipulate." Makima's smile never faltered. "Oh, I'm not trying to manipulate you, Jolyne. I'm just interested in getting to know you. We're not so different, you and I. We both have... unique abilities, and I think we could learn from each other." She took another step closer, her eyes locked onto Jolyne's like a predator sizing up its prey. Jolyne's grip on Stone Free tightened, and she began to unravel her body into a snaking mass of string. "You're not interested in me, Makima. You're interested in something else. Something that's mine." Her voice dropped to a growl, her words laced with warning. Makima's eyes never left Jolyne's face, but a flicker of curiosity danced in their depths. "Ah, you're right, Jolyne. I am interested in something that's yours. But I think we can come to a mutually beneficial arrangement. After all, I'm willing to offer you... protection." Her voice dripped with honey, but Jolyne saw the glint of steel beneath. The air seemed to vibrate with tension as the two women faced off. Jolyne's stringy form writhed and pulsed, ready to strike. Makima's eyes sparkled with a cold, calculating light, her smile still plastered on her face. "You're not as smart as you think you are, Makima," Jolyne sneered. "I can see right through you." Makima's smile never wavered. "Oh, I think you're wrong, Jolyne. I'm just getting started."
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] The piece establishes its premise competently but falls short of the prompt's specifications in notable ways. Most obviously, the prompt explicitly requests third person, present tense, yet the response is written entirely in past tense ("Jolyne navigated," "Makima chuckled"), a clear instruction violation. The length also falls short of the requested 1000 words, coming in around 600. Character work is serviceable but surface-level. Jolyne's suspicion of Makima is stated repeatedly rather than demonstrated through behavior or internal reasoning; we're told her "instincts prickled" and her "hackles rose" (used twice) without ever seeing the observational intelligence that makes Jolyne compelling. Her voice is generic-tough rather than authentically foul-mouthed and sharp — the prompt's flavor quote suggests a much more colorful register than the mild "Newsflash, sweetheart" delivered here. Makima fares slightly better: her honeyed menace and predatory patience are conveyed, and the "protection" offer is a plausible Makima move. However, her signature qualities — control, contracts, her fascination with specific powers — are only lightly gestured at. She reads as "generic manipulative villain" rather than the specific, chilling entity she is. The dialogue is the piece's weakest element. The exchange circles in place without escalation — the prompt asks for a battle of wits that "escalates dangerously," but the story ends at roughly the same tension level it began, with a stock cliffhanger line ("I'm just getting started") that could close any generic confrontation. No actual wit is deployed by either party; there's no move and countermove, no clever gambit, no dangerous turn. Prose is competent but cliché-heavy: "like honey dripping from a spoon," "like a gentle breeze on a summer day," "like a predator sizing up its prey," "dripping with disdain/honey/steel." These stock similes pile up and become distracting. The imagery of Stone Free unraveling is the most vivid moment, but it's underused — the Stand appears, writhes, and then nothing happens with it. Structurally, the piece is coherent and appropriately paced for a scene, but it's a scene, not a story — it has no arc, no turn, no payoff. The ending is neither earned darkness nor resolution; it simply stops. There are no unearned transformations or tonal inconsistencies, and the piece never becomes incoherent, but it also never rises above competent fan-fiction boilerplate. The emotional register is flat: tension is asserted rather than felt. + Scp: Notes from the Future — Score: 44.5
Prompt:
Write an SCP report about a researcher who begins finding handwritten notes from their future self, warning about a containment breach that hasn't happened yet. The notes become increasingly desperate and incoherent, sometimes containing information that contradicts known reality. Include the Foundation's attempts to determine if this is an actual temporal anomaly or psychological manipulation by a contained entity, and the researcher's struggle to trust their own perception. First person (researcher's perspective with interspersed notes), past tense. Length: 1000 words.
Model Output:
**Incident Report: Researcher's Temporal Anomaly** I still remember the day I first found the note. I was working late in the lab, trying to meet the deadline for my research project on anomalous temporal phenomena. As I was packing up my things to leave, I noticed a small piece of paper on my desk with a cryptic message scrawled on it: "Trust no one. Containment breach imminent. -J" At first, I thought it was just a prank from one of my colleagues, but as I looked closer, I realized the handwriting was unfamiliar. And then I saw the date at the top of the page: March 15, 2025. I was still three months away from that date. I tried to brush it off as a prank, but the words kept echoing in my mind. I began to feel a sense of unease, like I was being watched. Over the next few days, more notes started appearing on my desk, each one dated at a future date, always written in the same handwriting. **March 18, 2025** "Get out while you still can. They're watching you. Don't trust anyone. -J" I started to feel a creeping sense of paranoia. Who was "they"? And what did they want from me? I tried to focus on my work, but the notes continued to appear, each one more urgent and cryptic than the last. **March 22, 2025** "Project Echelon is compromised. Don't let them access the server. -J" I knew I had to report this to my superiors, but I was hesitant. What if it was just a prank? What if I was losing my mind? I decided to keep the notes to myself, hoping to gather more information before bringing it up. As the notes continued to appear, they became more and more desperate. The handwriting grew more erratic, the messages more disjointed. **April 5, 2025** "The sky is falling. Run. Run now. -J" I started to wonder if I was indeed losing my mind. Was I experiencing some kind of temporal anomaly, or was this some kind of psychological manipulation by a contained entity? I decided to keep a record of the notes, hoping to find some pattern or clue that could explain what was happening. **April 10, 2025** "The Foundation is not what you think it is. Trust me. -J" I began to feel like I was trapped in some kind of time loop, reliving the same events over and over. The notes seemed to be predicting events that hadn't happened yet, but the dates kept changing. I started to doubt my own perception of reality. **April 15, 2025** "The world will end in fire. I'm sorry. -J" I knew I had to get to the bottom of this. I scheduled a meeting with Dr. Patel, our department head, to report the notes and ask for her guidance. She listened attentively, her expression a mix of concern and skepticism. "I'm afraid this sounds like a classic case of temporal anxiety," she said. "We've seen cases where researchers experience temporal disorientation, where they begin to question their own perception of time and reality." But I knew what I saw. I knew the handwriting, the paper, the dates. I knew it was real. **April 20, 2025** "They're coming for you. Don't let them. -J" I started to feel like I was living in a nightmare, trapped in a never-ending cycle of fear and uncertainty. I began to question my own identity, wondering if I was the one writing the notes, or if I was just a pawn in some larger game. Dr. Patel ordered a full psychological evaluation, but I knew I wasn't crazy. I knew what I saw. **April 25, 2025** "The truth is in the numbers. Look for the pattern. -J" I spent hours poring over the notes, searching for a pattern or code that could explain the anomaly. But the more I searched, the more I realized that the notes were becoming increasingly incoherent. **May 1, 2025** "The cat is black. The sky is blue. -J" I felt like I was losing my grip on reality. Was I experiencing a temporal anomaly, or was I being manipulated by a contained entity? I didn't know what to believe. Dr. Patel called me into her office again, her expression serious. "Researcher, I think it's time we brought in some outside expertise. We'll run some additional tests, see if we can determine the source of these... messages." I nodded, feeling a mix of relief and trepidation. Was I finally getting to the bottom of this mystery, or was I walking into a trap? **May 5, 2025** "I'm still here. I'm still writing. -J" I looked up at Dr. Patel, hoping for answers. But she just shook her head. "We'll get to the bottom of this, Researcher. I promise." I left her office, feeling like I was trapped in a never-ending cycle of uncertainty. Was I a temporal anomaly, or was I just a pawn in some larger game? I didn't know, and I didn't know if I ever would. **Addendum:** I've included a copy of the notes, along with my own recordings and observations. I'm not sure what to make of it all. I'm still waiting for answers. **Note:** I've been instructed to keep this report confidential. I'm not sure if I'll be able to continue working on this project. The notes keep coming, and I'm starting to wonder if I'll ever be able to trust my own perception of reality. **Addendum 2:** I've been ordered to undergo additional psychological evaluation. I'm not sure if I'm ready to face what's coming.
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] The piece attempts the requested structure (first-person researcher, interspersed notes, past tense) but executes it in a flat, repetitive way. The notes themselves are generic and lack the escalating specificity or genuine incoherence that would make them chilling — "The cat is black. The sky is blue" is a limp stab at surrealism. The Foundation elements are thin: no SCP object designation, no containment procedures, no clinical framing; it reads as a generic workplace memo rather than an SCP report. The Foundation's investigation into whether this is temporal anomaly vs. psychological manipulation is gestured at via Dr. Patel, but she's a sketch and her dialogue ("temporal anxiety") is unconvincing and unprofessional-sounding. The researcher's paranoia is told repeatedly ("I began to question my own perception of reality") rather than shown through behavior or sensory detail. The prose is repetitive — the same doubts restated nearly verbatim multiple times ("Was I a temporal anomaly, or was I just a pawn..."). The ending trails off with addenda that add nothing. Emotionally flat, structurally meandering, and the twist potential (contradictory information, entity manipulation) is never developed. Some competence in pacing the note escalation, but little creativity or atmosphere. + Scp: Unmarked Doors Archive — Score: 44.3
Prompt:
Write an SCP entry about a series of identical unmarked doors appearing in abandoned buildings worldwide that lead to a massive, architecturally impossible archive. Documents within reference a previous global civilization destroyed by an unspecified containment failure. Foundation researchers discover references to their own organization under a different name, including personnel files for current staff dated centuries ago. Include exploration logs revealing sections of the archive actively being removed by entities resembling hazmat teams who flee when approached, and evidence that O5 Council members have visited the archive without authorization. Third person (exploration team perspective) with recovered document excerpts. Length: 1000 words.
Model Output:
**SCP-XXXX: "The Unmarked Doors"** **Object Class:** Euclid **Special Containment Procedures:** All instances of SCP-XXXX are to be sealed and marked with a bright orange warning stripe. Under no circumstances are any instances to be interacted with or attempted to be opened. Foundation personnel are prohibited from accessing SCP-XXXX without Level 3 clearance and explicit permission from at least two O5 Council members. **Description:** SCP-XXXX is a series of identical, unmarked doors that have appeared in abandoned buildings worldwide. These doors are made of a standard steel material, approximately 2.1 meters tall and 0.9 meters wide, with a standard doorknob and locking mechanism. Every instance of SCP-XXXX has been found to have identical scratches around the doorknob area. As of the most recent update, SCP-XXXX instances have been discovered in 37 countries across 5 continents, with the most recent discovery in Tokyo, Japan. All instances have been found in abandoned buildings, including factories, schools, hospitals, and residential areas. Upon further investigation, a massive, architecturally impossible archive has been discovered behind each door. The archive appears to be a labyrinthine collection of rooms and corridors that stretch seemingly infinitely in all directions. The archive is filled with documents, files, and artifacts from a previously unknown global civilization. **Recovered Document Excerpts:** **Document 1:** "The Incident Report" ...On the 40th day of the 11th month of the year 2187, a catastrophic containment failure occurred, resulting in the destruction of the entire city of New Eden. The exact cause of the failure is still unknown, but it is believed to have been related to the improper handling of Object designator 'Erebus-7'. The city was home to over 500,000 inhabitants, and the destruction caused widespread devastation and loss of life. The incident remains one of the darkest chapters in human history. **Document 2:** "Personnel File - Dr. Elliot Thompson" ...Name: Dr. Elliot Thompson Role: Lead Researcher, Project Erebus-7 Date of Birth: 11/02/2098 Date of Death: 04/12/2187 Dr. Thompson was a renowned expert in the field of exo-matter research. He was responsible for the development and implementation of Object designator 'Erebus-7'. His team was tasked with containing the object in a secure facility, but the containment failure resulted in the destruction of the city. **Document 3:** "Memorandum - O5 Council" ...To: All O5 Council members From: O5-1 Subject: Unauthorized Access to SCP-XXXX It has come to my attention that several O5 Council members have visited SCP-XXXX without authorization. I must stress that this is a serious breach of protocol and could compromise the integrity of our organization. I request that all O5 Council members refrain from accessing SCP-XXXX until further notice. **Exploration Logs:** **Log 1:** On//20, an exploration team led by Dr. Rachel Lee was dispatched to investigate an instance of SCP-XXXX in an abandoned factory in Moscow, Russia. Upon entering the archive, the team discovered a section of the labyrinthine collection of rooms and corridors that appeared to be actively being removed by entities resembling hazmat teams. The entities, later referred to as "SCP-XXXX-1", were wearing full hazmat suits and were carrying specialized equipment. When approached, the SCP-XXXX-1 entities fled the scene, leaving behind a trail of debris and scattered documents. **Log 2:** On//20, an exploration team led by Dr. John Taylor was dispatched to investigate an instance of SCP-XXXX in an abandoned school in Buenos Aires, Argentina. Upon entering the archive, the team discovered a section of the labyrinthine collection of rooms and corridors that appeared to be actively being used by Foundation personnel. The team found personnel files for current staff, dated centuries ago, including personnel files for O5 Council members. The files contained information on the development of the Foundation and its early experiments. **Addendum:** Following the discovery of the personnel files, O5 Council members have been restricted from accessing SCP-XXXX until further notice. All exploration teams have been instructed to exercise extreme caution when approaching SCP-XXXX instances. **Incident Report:** On//20, an incident occurred when an exploration team led by Dr. Emily Chen attempted to access a section of the archive that had been previously identified as containing sensitive information. The team was met with resistance from SCP-XXXX-1 entities, who attacked the team with specialized equipment. The team was forced to retreat, but not before Dr. Chen was gravely injured. The incident remains under investigation, and the team's findings are still being analyzed. **Conclusion:** SCP-XXXX is a complex and mysterious object that poses a significant threat to the Foundation and global security. Further research is required to understand the purpose and origin of the archive, as well as the nature of the SCP-XXXX-1 entities. All instances of SCP-XXXX are to be sealed and marked with a bright orange warning stripe. Under no circumstances are any instances to be interacted with or attempted to be opened. Foundation personnel are prohibited from accessing SCP-XXXX without Level 3 clearance and explicit permission from at least two O5 Council members. **Recommendations:** 1. Establish a research team to investigate the purpose and origin of the archive. 2. Develop a strategy to safely contain and study SCP-XXXX-1 entities. 3. Conduct further research on the personnel files and their relevance to the development of the Foundation. **Authorization:** This document has been approved for release by O5-1.
Judge Evaluation:
[Analysis] The response follows the SCP format and hits most of the requested elements: unmarked doors in abandoned buildings, an impossible archive, references to a prior civilization destroyed by a containment failure, personnel files for current staff dated centuries ago, hazmat entities removing sections who flee when approached, and O5 unauthorized visits. However, it does so in a flat, checklist manner with little atmosphere or craft. Strengths: structural adherence to SCP conventions (Object Class, Containment Procedures, logs, addenda), and inclusion of nearly all prompt elements. The premise itself has inherent intrigue. Weaknesses: The prose is mechanical and report-like without the clinical-voice polish that makes good SCP entries work. Dates are inconsistent and sloppy ("On//20" placeholders left in, a civilization destroyed in 2187 described as "one of the darkest chapters in human history" while personnel files are "dated centuries ago" relative to the present). The "previous global civilization" is barely developed — the documents read like generic incident reports rather than eerie recovered artifacts. The hazmat entities are mentioned but never shown vividly; the exploration logs are summaries, not logs, with no dialogue, timestamps, or sensory detail. The O5 memorandum is oddly mundane and undercuts the mystery rather than deepening it. The ending repeats containment procedures verbatim and adds bureaucratic "Recommendations," draining all tension. There is no escalation, no unsettling implication left to linger — the prompt's most chilling idea (Foundation staff existing centuries ago) is stated and then abandoned. The piece is coherent and readable but emotionally inert, uncreative in execution, and amateurish in its inconsistencies and placeholder text.
